# Micro-benchmark results

Every case behind the [micro-benchmark summary](performance.md#micro-benchmarks):
dotnet/performance's runtime micro-benchmarks, with its `System.Numerics`
vector, `System.Runtime.Intrinsics` and `System.Memory` benchmarks, run on
NetWasm, Mono AOT and desktop .NET 11 with the versions and settings on that
page. Each time is nanoseconds per call of the benchmark method, the fastest of
two interleaved rounds. The ratios are NetWasm's time over the other runtime's;
lower is faster.

| Family | Cases | NetWasm / Mono AOT | NetWasm / desktop .NET 11 JIT |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Runtime | 384 | 0.56 | 0.85 |
| Math | 96 | 0.85 | 1.41 |
| `System.Memory` spans | 364 | 0.59 | 2.34 |
| `System.Numerics` vectors | 770 | 0.39 | 3.18 |
| `System.Runtime.Intrinsics` | 588 | 0.35 | 3.29 |
| All | 2,202 | 0.45 | 2.34 |

Five cases never use what they compute: `Benchstone.BenchF`'s `NewtE`, `Trap`
and `Simpsn` return a constant, and two `LowLevelPerf` cases create delegates
they drop. Mono AOT's LLVM removes the work and times an empty loop, so their
ratios against Mono AOT run from about 3,000 to millions. NetWasm and desktop
.NET do the work. They stay in the geometric means.
